The registry REFUSES to forget a LIVE session, so this can\n    // never orphan a supervised process ù it throws, and the launch is refused.\n    forgetSession: (paneId) => { if (manager && manager.registry.has(paneId)) manager.forget(paneId); },\n    // The supervised spawn + its post-spawn ROLLBACK (spec-audit MAJOR-3) live in picker/pane-wiring.js\n    // so the headless suite drives them: written here they were correct and untestable, and reverting\n    // the rollback left every suite green (spec-audit MAJOR-2). Bound per call rather than once,\n    // because `manager` is assigned during bootstrap: capturing it at launcher-construction time\n    // would freeze whatever it was then, and a null capture would fail as a TypeError rather than as\n    // the module\'s own fail-closed refusal.\n    spawnSession: (args) => createGovernedPaneSpawn({\n      manager, panes, persistLayoutSnapshot, pushState, emitLayoutNow, log,\n    })(args),\n    // A spawn result is not itself proof that supervision remains registered. Re-observe the exact\n    // pid/generation after birth; worker-spawn records `supervised:false` when this is absent/stale.\n    processIdentity: (paneId) => (manager ? manager.processIdentity(paneId) : null),\n    augmentSpawnEnv: (env, ctx) => {\n      if (!sovereignControl || !sovereignControl.port) {\n        throw new Error("Sovereign application-control MCP gateway is not ready");\n      }\n      const chrome = (ctx.ticket && ctx.ticket.chrome) || {};\n      return sovereignControl.childEnv({\n        node_id: ctx.identity.node_id, role: "worker", project_id: "proj",\n        provider_id: chrome.provider || null, model_id: chrome.model_slug || null,\n        pane_id: ctx.paneId, session_id: ctx.sessionId,\n        store_root: path.join(REPO_ROOT, ".sovereign_store"),\n      }, env).env;\n    },\n    revokeNodeControl: (nodeId) => { if (sovereignControl) sovereignControl.revokeNode(nodeId); },\n    onChange: () => pushState(),\n    log,\n  });\n  return workerLauncherInstance;\n}\n\n/** The observable per-pane worker launch state (invariant 27) ù read by the self-check and the UI. */\nfunction workerLaunchState(paneId) { return workerLauncher().record(paneId); }\n\n/**\n * A governed worker pane\'s session ended (exit, operator close, or the fail-closed kill on\n * supervision loss): its chrome must say so. The model badge is KEPT ù the operator picked it and it\n * is still what that pane last ran ù but `node_state`/`governed`/`pid` describe a node that no\n * longer exists, so they are corrected here and the corrected chrome is what the layout snapshot\n * (and therefore the next boot\'s recovery view) carries.\n */\nfunction markWorkerPaneChromeEnded(event) {\n  const id = event && event.id;\n  // The RULE is `endedWorkerChrome` in picker/pane-wiring.js (pure, headlessly tested); this is the\n  // wiring only. It was written inline here and no test or receipt leg could see it ù reverting it\n  // left every suite green (spec-audit MAJOR-2).\n  const ended = endedWorkerChrome(id ? paneChrome.get(id) : null, event || {});\n  if (!ended) return;\n  paneChrome.set(id, ended);\n  persistLayoutSnapshot();\n  // àand the RENDERER\'s copy, or the operator\'s badge keeps reading `live` for a dead pane while the\n  // shell knows better ù the server-side correction alone would fix only what a restart shows.\n  if (win && !win.isDestroyed()) win.webContents.send("pane:chrome", id, ended);\n  pushState();\n  pushConductor();\n}\n\n/**\n * A picker selection \u2192 a LIVE governed worker session in a pane (Phase 17B `.spawn`).\n *\n * 16B recorded the selection, badged the pane and started nothing ù the operator\'s finding F3. The\n * authority is unchanged and still Python\'s: `emit_worker_launch.py` runs the whole gate chain and\n * either issues an executable ticket or refuses. This handler executes an issued ticket and records\n * a refusal verbatim; it self-authorizes nothing (invariant 1) and never spawns a session that was\n * not authorized and admitted (invariant 2).\n */\nasync function spawnFromSelection(selection) {\n  // W-37: the guard is handed the LIVE host enumeration so it can corroborate the selection rather\n  // than believe it ù the same `lastPickerModel` the conductor pre-launch path and\n  // `hostVerifiedFlag` already consult.\n  const reason = refuseSelection(selection, {\n    conductorPaneId,\n    hostOptions: (lastPickerModel && lastPickerModel.picker && lastPickerModel.picker.options) || [],\n  });\n  if (reason) {\n    log(`picker: spawn REFUSED (fail-closed) ù ${reason}`);\n    return { recorded: false, launched: false, error: reason };\n  }\n  const o = selection.option;\n  const auth = (lastPickerModel && lastPickerModel.picker && lastPickerModel.picker.authorization) || null;\n  const paneId = resolveTargetPaneId(selection);\n  log(`picker: operator selected ${o.provider}/${o.label} role=${selection.role} `\n    + `mode=${selection.mode} target=${paneId} ù requesting a governed launch`);\n  const res = await workerLauncher().launchWorkerPane({\n    paneId,\n    selection: { option: o, role: selection.role, mode: selection.mode },\n  });\n  const rec = res.record || {};\n  // The LAUNCHED pane\'s chrome is the ticket\'s (Python\'s), with the one fact this shell owns added:\n  // the session is running and this is its pid. A non-launched pane gets the honest preview naming\n  // the outcome. Either way the chrome is recorded server-side keyed by paneId so paneMeta() \u2192 the\n  // layout snapshot \u2192 a restart repaints the exact badge (16D `.recovery`, U68).\n  //\n  // A refusal that left a LIVE session untouched (`heldSessionUntouched` ù the operator clicked the\n  // picker on a pane that is already running one) must NOT repaint that pane: writing the refusal\n  // chrome there reported a live, supervised, Python-authorized node as `launch_refused` /\n  // `governed:false`, persisted it into the last-good layout, and repainted the badge ù a running\n  // node described as dark (validator MAJOR-1, invariants 3/27). The refusal is still returned in\n  // full; the pane keeps the chrome of what it is actually running.\n  const chrome = res.launched\n    ? { ...(rec.chrome || {}), node_state: "running", governed: true, pid: rec.pid || null,\n      session_generation: rec.sessionGeneration,\n      launch_reason: null, launch_refused_by: null }\n    : res.heldSessionUntouched\n      ? (paneChrome.get(paneId) || null)\n      : chromePreview(selection, auth, {\n        nodeState: rec.state === "refused" ? "launch_refused"\n          : rec.state === "failed" ? "launch_failed" : "launch_unavailable",\n        reason: res.reason || null, refusedBy: res.refusedBy || null,\n      });\n  if (panes.panes.has(paneId) && !res.heldSessionUntouched) {\n    paneChrome.set(paneId, chrome);\n    persistLayoutSnapshot(); // capture the badge into the LAST GOOD layout for the next boot\n  }\n  // The identity fields describe THIS attempt. On the `heldSessionUntouched` path the launcher\n  // deliberately returns the RUNNING session\'s record (that is how it proves it left it alone), so\n  // reporting `rec.sessionId`/`nodeId`/`argv`/`state` here would hand the caller another session\'s\n  // identity labelled as this click\'s result ù `launched:false` next to `state:"running"`\n  // (spec-audit MINOR-5). This attempt started nothing and has no identity; it says so, and the\n  // untouched session is reported under its own name so the fact is not lost either.\n  const own = res.heldSessionUntouched ? null : rec;\n  return {\n    recorded: true, launched: res.launched === true, chrome, target: paneId,\n    state: own ? own.state || null : null,\n    sessionId: own ? own.sessionId || null : null,\n    nodeId: own ? own.nodeId || null : null,\n    argv: own ? own.argv || null : null,\n    heldSessionUntouched: res.heldSessionUntouched === true,\n    heldSessionId: res.heldSessionUntouched ? rec.sessionId || null : null,\n    reason: res.reason || null, refusedBy: res.refusedBy || null,\n    error: res.launched ? null : (res.reason || null),\n    note: res.launched\n      ? "LIVE governed worker session running in the pane under a Python-issued launch ticket "\n        + "(supervised admission, workspace cwd, credential-scrubbed env, I-X3 counted for a frontier "\n        + "model / VRAM-residency admitted for a local one)"\n      : "no session was started ù the governed reason is carried verbatim (fail closed)",\n  };\n}\n\n// ---- standard MCP application-control surface ------------------------------\n// Role checks, deadline state and the HTTP application-control handlers are built below from the\n// require-able control/application-control.js module. What remains here is only live shell binding.\n\nfunction operationalDispatchSummary() {\n  return operationalState.operationalDispatchSummary();\n}\n\nconst operationalState = createOperationalState({\n  repoRoot: REPO_ROOT,\n  workerRecords: () => (workerLauncherInstance ? workerLauncher().records() : []),\n  chromeFor: (paneId) => paneChrome.get(paneId),\n  mcpState: (nodeId) => (sovereignControl\n    ? sovereignControl.connectionState(nodeId) : { state: "disconnected", last_seen: null }),\n  sessionEstablished,\n  normalizedProvider,\n  conductorLaunch: () => conductorLaunch,\n  conductorDescriptor,\n  conductorPaneId: () => conductorPaneId,\n});\nconst {\n  liveWorkerRecords, operationalConductorStatus, operationalNodeStatus, workerRecordFor,\n} = operationalState;\n\nasync function waitForNodeMcp(nodeId, timeoutMs = 120000) {\n  const deadline = Date.now() + timeoutMs;\n  while (Date.now() < deadline) {\n    if (sovereignControl && sovereignControl.connectionState(nodeId).state === "connected") return true;\n    await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 250));\n  }\n  return false;\n}\n\n// U329 (unit 19.4): the ONE reader readiness may use. `paneScreen()` ù which returned\n// `buffer.snapshot()`, the whole 256 KB scrollback ù is gone: it is what made a dismissed overlay\n// permanent and what let the shell\'s own echoed objective classify the worker it was sent to. This\n// hands the module the RingBuffer itself so the bound is `sliceFrom()`\'s, fail-closed, and no caller\n// can widen it by passing a bigger string.\nconst readinessWindow = createScreenWindow({\n  bufferFor: (paneId) => (manager && manager.registry.has(paneId)\n    ? manager.registry.get(paneId).buffer : null),\n});\n\nconst pause = (ms) => new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));\n\n// U328: everything the SYSTEM types into a pane goes through here. The two bindings that could be\n// got wrong (is the screen readable at all; which provider\'s rules apply) are the module\'s, because\n// this file cannot be required in a test (U338) ù a hand-rolled reader that always claimed the\n// screen was legible would open the gate for every pane with nothing able to see it.\n// U373 residual (unit 19.4-followon): the gate\'s own read is the SAME bounded window object\n// readiness classifies through ù `readinessWindow`, one line up, passed by reference. Callers that own an\n *  operational state consult this so a withheld write is reported as the provider state it is. */\nfunction paneWriteRefusalFor(paneId) {\n  return paneWriter.refusalFor(paneId);\n}\n\nfunction setWorkerOperationalState(paneId, operationalState, patch = {}) {\n  const record = workerLauncher().updateRecord(paneId, { operationalState, ...patch });\n  const chrome = { ...(paneChrome.get(paneId) || record.chrome || {}), node_state: operationalState };\n  paneChrome.set(paneId, chrome);\n  if (win && !win.isDestroyed()) win.webContents.send("pane:chrome", paneId, chrome);\n  pushState();\n  pushConductor();\n  return record;\n}\n\n/** The structured failure the DEADLINE paths report (`worker_task_soft/hard_deadline`). "spawned" : "from env"} on 127.0.0.1:${gw.port}`);\n  const client = new IpcClient({ host: "127.0.0.1", port: gw.port, nodeId: NODE_ID, token: gw.token, key: gw.key });\n  // A SEPARATE read channel for the inspector, on the same loopback credential. The IpcClient\n  // control channel is strictly sequential (one request in flight); the supervisor already owns\n  // `client` for its 5 s health heartbeat + session_event notifies, so the inspector\'s 8-status\n  // sweep gets its own connection rather than contending with (and being refused by) the\n  // heartbeat. The gateway is threaded and authenticates per-connection, so a second channel for\n  // the same node identity is legitimate. It connects lazily on the first inspector:fetch.\n  ipcClient = new IpcClient({ host: "127.0.0.1", port: gw.port, nodeId: NODE_ID, token: gw.token, key: gw.key });\n  // NOTE (Phase 16D `.statusbar`): the status bar no longer rides a dedicated IPC channel ù the\n  // shell\'s EchoControlSurface gateway does not expose subscription_status, so the count is sourced\n  // from the real governor via the bounded emitter (statusbar:fetch \u2192 governor-source). The IPC\n  // surface (SubscriptionStatusControlSurface) remains the design endpoint for a live governor-backed\n  // gateway at 16F; statusbar/source.js + its test stay as that endpoint\'s proven data path.\n  supervisor = new IpcSupervisor({\n    client,\n    // loss edge: fail closed ù tear every session down, no naked session survives the gap.\n    onSupervisionLost: () => { if (manager) manager.killAll(); pushState(); },\n    // restore edge: the channel re-verified on a new epoch ù the UI recovers (re-push chrome +\n    // recompute the º10.2 layout). Re-admission of new sessions is now open again.\n    onSupervisionRestored: () => { pushState(); emitLayoutNow(); },\n    log,\n  });\n  manager = new SessionManager({ ptyFactory, supervisor });\n  manager.onEvent((event) => {\n    if (event.kind === "data") {\n      // Tag every chunk with a per-session seq (registry.feed already ran in the session-manager\n      // before this emit, so the scrollback snapshot and this seq stay in lockstep). The renderer\n      // buffers live chunks until it has replayed scrollback, then drops chunks <= the snapshot\n      // boundary ù closing the blank-pane defect (banner printed before the term view existed).\n      const seq = (dataSeq.get(event.id) || 0) + 1;\n      dataSeq.set(event.id, seq);\n      if (win && !win.isDestroyed()) win.webContents.send("pane:data", event.id, event.data.toString("utf8"), seq);\n    }\n    // Phase 17A `.pty`: the live conductor session ending (its own exit, an operator close, or the\n    // fail-closed killAll on supervision loss) hands the durable I-X3 terminal back ù D-LOOP-1, and\n    // the operator\'s own subscription is not held by a session that no longer exists.\n    if (event.kind === "exit" || event.kind === "kill") {\n      trackSessionRelease(onConductorSessionEnded(event), "conductor");\n      const endedWorker = workerLauncher().record(event.id);\n      if (endedWorker && endedWorker.nodeId) clearNodeDeadlines(endedWorker.nodeId);\n      // Phase 17B `.spawn` / D-LOOP-1: the same rule for a governed WORKER pane ù a frontier worker\n      // session that ended must not keep holding 1 of the operator\'s 2 durable terminals. A local\n      // worker holds none and the release reclaims nothing; it is called anyway so the lifecycle has\n      // one shape. Never throws into the event loop.\n      trackSessionRelease(workerLauncher().onWorkerSessionEnded(event), "worker");\n      // àand the pane\'s CHROME has to stop claiming a running governed node the moment there is not\n      // one. It was written once, at launch, and never revised: after an ordinary exit ù or after\n      // the fail-closed `killAll` on supervision loss, i.e. exactly when supervision is GONE ù\n      // `paneMeta()` still reported `node_state:"running", governed:true`, and every later layout\n      // snapshot baked that into the state a restart replays (spec-audit MAJOR-1, invariants 3/27).\n      markWorkerPaneChromeEnded(event);\n    }\n    // persist the append-only lifecycle log on every state-changing event so a full shell\n    // restart can reconstruct what existed (best-effort; a write failure never breaks a session).\n    if (["spawn", "exit", "kill", "refused"].includes(event.kind)) {\n      recoveryStore.persist(manager.registry.eventLog());\n      persistLayoutSnapshot(); // keep the layout snapshot in step with the session log for recovery\n      pushState();\n    }\n  });\n\n  // Cross-restart reconstruction: fold the persisted layout snapshot + the session view + the\n  // current admission state into the conductor-first layout the shell rebuilds (º15E .recovery /\n  // OP-7 º12.4). Fail-closed ù the pinned CONDUCTOR is always pane 1; interrupted worker panes are\n  // surfaced for SUPERVISED relaunch, NEVER auto-respawned (invariant 2); nothing is admitted until\n  // the channel re-verifies. The actual pane-1 CONDUCTOR is (re)placed by createConductorPane() on\n  // did-finish-load; this payload gives the renderer the recovered structure to draw.\n  const recovered = reconstructConductorFirstLayout();\n  const interrupted = recovered.interrupted;\n  if (interrupted.length) {\n    log(`recovery: ${interrupted.length} interrupted pane(s)/session(s) from a prior run ù supervised relaunch required (no naked re-spawn)`);\n  }\n  log(`recovery: conductor-first layout reconstructed ù CONDUCTOR pane 1 + ${recovered.summary.workers} worker pane(s), ${interrupted.length} needing relaunch, admission ${recovered.admissionOpen ? "OPEN" : "SHUT (awaiting channel)"}`);\n  if (win && !win.isDestroyed()) win.webContents.send("shell:recovery", { layout: recovered, interrupted: recoveryStore.loadInterrupted() });\n\n  const ready = await supervisor.start();\n  log(ready ? "supervision READY (verified control-plane channel)" : "supervision NOT ready ù sessions refused until the channel verifies");\n  await ensureSovereignControl();\n}\n\n/**\n * The operator\'s keyboard, as ELECTRON MAIN sees it (`before-input-event` fires before the page does).\n *\n * Two gestures, and the second is the one that matters (Phase 17C `.disarm`, U166). The explicit\n * Ctrl+Shift+Escape chord was the ONLY way an admitted voice turn ever ended, and it appears in no\n * chrome, no string and no operator document ù so in practice the first spoken utterance of a session\n * muted the operator\'s own typing until the session was killed. Ordinary typing now ends the turn:\n * everything the CLI does between an admitted spoken prompt and the operator\'s next keystroke is\n * treated as voice-originated and non-executing, and the operator taking the keyboard back is the\n * bound. The chord remains for ending it WITHOUT typing into the pane.\n *\n * Only the chord is consumed; an ordinary keystroke must still reach the pane the operator is typing\n * into ù main disarms first and the character goes on to the CLI, in that order, so the prompt it\n * begins is admitted rather than blocked.\n */\nfunction handleOperatorResumeInput(event, input) {\n  if (shouldOperatorResumeVoiceTurn(input)) {\n    event.preventDefault(); // this authority gesture is consumed by main, never typed into the CLI\n    conductorVoiceAuthority.disarm("electron_main_operator_chord", { chord: "Ctrl+Shift+Escape" });\n    return true;\n  }\n  if (isOperatorTypedKey(input)) {\n    // `key_kind` only ù an append-only audit that reached evidence receipts and recorded WHICH keys\n    // the operator pressed would be a keylogger, which no invariant asks for.\n    const kind = operatorKeyKind(input);\n    conductorVoiceAuthority.disarm("electron_main_before_input_event", { key_kind: kind });\n    // W-39: the residue block lifts HERE, on the OS input path, and nowhere else. The operator\n    // taking the input line back is what resolves it ù whatever an unsubmitted voice utterance left\n    // there is now theirs to see and edit. Gated on the two RESOLVING kinds (Enter, or a Ctrl/Alt/\n    // Meta chord such as Ctrl-C): clearing on any operator key would discard the block the moment a\n    // single character was typed, which is the same defect with a different trigger.\n    //\n    // The existing `operatorKeyKind` classification carries it. No new provenance flag, and\n    // `operatorInputResolvesResidue` is untouched ù that predicate was always correct; it was being\n    // asked from a channel the child can reach.\n    if (kind === "submit" || kind === "control-combination") {\n      clearConductorInputResidue("the operator submitted or cancelled the conductor input");\n    }\n  }\n  return false;\n}\n\n/**\n * W-30: the ONE navigation this shell ever performs is its own `loadFile`. Everything else is an\n * escape. `file://` is the permitted set, which is the review\'s recommended shape ù and its limit is\n * recorded rather than described away: a navigation to some OTHER local file is still permitted. The\n * renderer stays sandboxed with `contextIsolation`, so the value of that to an attacker is low, and\n * the advisory class being closed here is escape to REMOTE content.\n */\nconst navigationIsPermitted = (url) => String(url || "").startsWith("file://");\n\nfunction makeWindow() {\n  win = new BrowserWindow({\n    width: 1600, height: 950, title: "Sovereign Orchestration Workspace",\n    backgroundColor: "#0b0e14",\n    webPreferences: { preload: path.join(__dirname, "preload.js"), contextIsolation: true, nodeIntegration: false, sandbox: true },\n  });\n  // The child owns the hook bearer and the renderer is least-trusted, so neither may end a voice\n  // turn. Only what Electron main observes on the OS input path is operator-authenticated: the\n  // operator\'s ORDINARY typing (17C `.disarm`/U166 ù this is what gives the restriction an end, and\n  // it is not scoped to the conductor pane; see U170) or the explicit Ctrl+Shift+Escape chord, which\n  // ends it without typing anything into the CLI.\n  win.webContents.on("before-input-event", (event, input) => {\n    handleOperatorResumeInput(event, input);\n  });\n  // Under SHELL_SELFCHECK, pass a load-time query flag so the renderer arms its read-only\n  // self-check observability hook ONLY for the diagnostic run ù it is absent in a normal operator\n  // launch (defense-in-depth; the hook grants no authority regardless).\n  // Phase 17C `.mic`: the renderer needs the MICROPHONE for push-to-talk ù and nothing else at all.\n  // Electron\'s default is to grant every permission a page asks for; the renderer is the least-trusted\n  // surface (invariant 29) and this shell renders untrusted PTY output into its DOM, so the default is\n  // replaced by an explicit allow-list.\n  //\n  // The allow-list is of one CAPABILITY, not one string. Electron\'s `media` permission covers audio AND\n  // video, discriminated by `details.mediaTypes` ù granting it wholesale would hand the sandbox the\n  // operator\'s webcam, which no invariant asks for and which the STT-only scope (invariant 24) excludes.\n  // A `media` request that mentions video is refused outright rather than partially satisfied.\n  // GRANTS are logged as well as denials: a capability the operator cannot see being taken is not\n  // observable state (invariant 27).\n  const allowMedia = (permission, details) => {\n    if (permission !== "media" && permission !== "audioCapture") return false;\n    const types = (details && details.mediaTypes) || null;\n    if (Array.isArray(types) && types.length) return types.every((t) => t === "audio");\n    return permission === "audioCapture";   // no declared types \u21d2 only the unambiguous audio permission\n  };\n  const decide = (permission, details) => {\n    const granted = allowMedia(permission, details);\n    log(`renderer permission ${granted ? "GRANTED" : "DENIED"}: ${permission}`\n      + `${details && details.mediaTypes ? ` [${details.mediaTypes.join(",")}]` : ""}`);\n    return granted;\n  };\n  win.webContents.session.setPermissionRequestHandler((_wc, permission, callback, details) => callback(decide(permission, details)));\n  // The synchronous check path (`navigator.permissions.query`, device-label enumeration) has its own\n  // handler and otherwise falls back to Electron\'s permissive default ù the same rule applies to both.\n  win.webContents.session.setPermissionCheckHandler((_wc, permission, _origin, details) => allowMedia(permission, details));\n  // W-30, the two escapes Electron leaves open by default (R-04: NEITHER was present).\n  // `window.open` is denied outright ù there is no browsing surface in this app (zero occurrences\n  // repo-wide) so an allow-list would be inventing one. `will-navigate` is the top-level half: the\n  // CSP is `script-src \'self\'` and `default-src` does NOT constrain navigation, so without this the\n  // renderer can navigate the window to remote content and nothing stops it.\n  win.webContents.setWindowOpenHandler(() => ({ action: "deny" }));\n  win.webContents.on("will-navigate", (event, url) => {\n    if (navigationIsPermitted(url)) return;\n    event.preventDefault();\n    // Invariant 27: a refusal must be visible. The SCHEME only ù a URL from a compromised renderer\n    // is attacker-chosen text and may carry a token in its query string, and º2.2\'s rule is that we\n    // log names, never values.\n    const scheme = (/^[a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*:/i.exec(String(url || "")) || ["(no scheme)"])[0];\n    log(`renderer navigation DENIED: ${scheme}`);\n  });\n  win.loadFile(path.join(__dirname, "renderer", "index.html"),\n    process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK ? { query: { selfcheck: "1" } } : undefined);\n  if (process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK) {\n    // surface renderer console + crashes to the inherited stdout so the self-check is diagnosable\n    // only warnings/errors (level >= 2) ù logging info/log would feed back through S.onLog \u2192 console.log\n    win.webContents.on("console-message", (_e, level, message, line, sourceId) => {\n      if (level >= 2) log(`[renderer:${level}] ${message} (${sourceId}:${line})`);\n    });\n    win.webContents.on("render-process-gone", (_e, details) => log(`[renderer] gone: ${JSON.stringify(details)}`));\n    win.webContents.on("did-fail-load", (_e, code, desc) => log(`[renderer] did-fail-load ${code} ${desc}`));\n  }\n  // Debounced relayout channel (Plan º10.2): onLayout sends the plan the renderer reflows to.\n  layoutSched = new LayoutScheduler({ onLayout: (plan) => { if (win && !win.isDestroyed()) win.webContents.send("shell:layout", plan); } });\n  win.webContents.on("did-finish-load", () => {\n    createConductorPane(); // conductor-first: pane 1 is the pinned CONDUCTOR node (º12.4)\n    pushState();\n    pushApprovals();       // seed the badge from the cached model immediately (honest 0 until sourced)\n    // Phase 17D `.events`: start THIS run\'s approval log empty. A pending row names a broker\n    // classification and a session that do not survive the process, so carrying yesterday\'s rows into\n    // today\'s drawer would re-create finding F2 one restart later. What the operator sees at launch is\n    // what this run has produced: nothing, yet.\n    // ONCE per process, not once per `did-finish-load`: this handler also fires on a renderer reload,\n    // and truncating there would silently discard approvals the operator had pending in a session that\n    // never ended. The window reloading is not the session restarting.\n    if (!sessionApprovalsStarted) { sessionApprovalsStarted = true; sessionApprovals.begin(); }\n    // Source the drawer from that log in the background (a `py -3.12` start must not sit on first\n    // paint), then re-push the badge. Fail-closed + bounded.\n    refreshApprovalModel().then(() => pushApprovals()).catch((e) => log(`approvals: initial source failed (fail-closed empty drawer): ${e && e.message}`));\n    // Phase 17C `.probe` (U74): start the STT-engine probe in the BACKGROUND at first paint. It costs\n    // 7û18 s on this host (measured) and is allowed up to 90 s, so it must never sit on the paint path\n    // ù until it answers the chrome honestly reads "probingà". When it lands, push the state so the\n    // badge flips itself with no operator action. `.start()` never rejects (faults settle as\n    // `unavailable` with the reason named), but the catch stays: a background task must not be able to\n    // raise an unhandled rejection in the shell.\n    ensureVoiceProbe().start()\n      .then((s) => { log(`voice: STT engine probe \u2192 ${s.state} (${s.elapsedMs}ms) ù ${s.reason}`); pushVoice(); })\n      .catch((e) => log(`voice: STT engine probe failed unexpectedly: ${e && e.message}`));\n    // Phase 17C `.mic` / invariant 26: construct the capture store AT STARTUP so its purge actually\n    // runs at startup. Built lazily on first capture, the "startup purge" only ran when the operator\n    // next used voice ù so recorded speech orphaned by a crash could survive weeks of sessions in\n    // which they never pressed talk, while the code claimed a one-session bound.\n    try { ensureCaptureStore(); } catch (e) { log(`voice: capture store unavailable: ${e && e.message}`); }\n    emitLayoutNow();\n    if (rendererReadyResolve) rendererReadyResolve(); // release the self-check gate\n  });\n}\n\nfunction teardown() {\n  // Do not reset/stop voice authority here: before-quit is a kill REQUEST, not proof that the\n  // conductor PTY is gone. SessionManager\'s onExit path resets it when confirmed; if Electron exits\n  // first, loss of this authenticated loopback service is fail-closed in the child hook.\n  // Phase 17C `.probe` / D-LOOP-1: abandon an in-flight STT probe. It runs 7û22 s on this host (up to\n  // its 90 s budget), so quitting inside that window would otherwise orphan the emitter.\n  try { if (voiceProbe) voiceProbe.dispose(); } catch { /* teardown must never throw */ }\n  // Phase 17C `.mic` / invariant 26: leave no recorded speech behind. `withCapture` already deletes on\n  // every normal path; this catches a quit that raced an in-flight transcription.\n  try { if (captureStore) captureStore.purgeOwned(); } catch { /* teardown must never throw */ }\n  try { if (stopCaptureJanitor) stopCaptureJanitor(); } catch { /* teardown must never throw */ }\n  // Signal every PTY before considering any lease release. The synchronous kill event moves the\n  // conductor to `terminating`; only node-pty\'s later onExit can clear authority and release its\n  // durable terminal. If Electron exits first, dead-holder reaping observes the vanished owner.\n  try { manager && manager.killAll(); } catch { /* ignore */ }\n  // Never release an I-X3 terminal on quit intent. If node-pty confirms exit before Electron leaves,\n  // the normal exact-generation callback releases it; otherwise the durable ledger reaps this holder\n  // only after the Electron process itself is actually gone.\n  // U334 (unit 19.7): the authenticated gateway is torn down HERE, with every other resource, and no\n  // longer only as a bare `await` two functions later. `stop()` is bounded and idempotent, so\n  // starting it at kill-request time revokes every node credential immediately while the quit path\'s\n  // `await` still receives the same outcome. Fire-and-forget on purpose: teardown() is synchronous\n  // and must never throw ù the awaiting caller is where the result is read.\n  try { if (sovereignControl) sovereignControl.stop().catch(() => {}); } catch { /* teardown must never throw */ }\n  try { layoutSched && layoutSched.dispose(); } catch { /* ignore */ }\n  try { ipcClient && ipcClient.close(); } catch { /* ignore */ }\n  try { supervisor && supervisor.stop(); } catch { /* ignore */ }\n  try { gatewayProc && gatewayProc.kill(); } catch { /* ignore */ }\n}\n\nfunction waitForChildExit(proc, timeoutMs = 15000) {\n  if (!proc || proc.exitCode !== null || proc.signalCode !== null) return Promise.resolve(true);\n  return new Promise((resolve) => {\n    let settled = false;\n    const finish = (exited) => {\n      if (settled) return;\n      settled = true;\n      clearTimeout(timer);\n      proc.off("exit", onExit);\n      resolve(exited);\n    };\n    const onExit = () => finish(true);\n    const timer = setTimeout(() => finish(false), timeoutMs);\n    proc.once("exit", onExit);\n  });\n}\n\nasync function teardownSelfCheck() {\n  teardown();\n  const sessions = manager\n    ? await manager.shutdown(15000)\n    : { complete: true, pending: [] };\n  const releases = await waitForSessionReleases(15000);\n  const heldWorkers = workerLauncher().heldSessions();\n  const conductorHeld = conductorLaunch.leaseId ? [conductorLaunch.sessionId] : [];\n  let voiceStop = null;\n  if (sessions.complete && releases.complete && heldWorkers.length === 0 && conductorHeld.length === 0) {\n    voiceStop = await conductorVoiceAuthority.stop();\n  }\n  // teardown() above already started this; the memoised promise is what returns here (U334).\n  const controlStop = sovereignControl ? await sovereignControl.stop() : null;\n  const gatewayExited = await waitForChildExit(gatewayProc);\n  const controlComplete = !controlStop || controlStop.closed === true;\n  const voiceComplete = !voiceStop || voiceStop.closed === true;\n  return {\n    complete: sessions.complete && releases.complete && heldWorkers.length === 0\n      && conductorHeld.length === 0 && gatewayExited && controlComplete && voiceComplete,\n    sessions, releases, heldWorkers, conductorHeld, gatewayExited, controlStop, voiceStop,\n  };\n}\n\nlet normalQuitStarted = false;\nasync function completeNormalQuit(faultKind = null) {\n  teardown();\n  const sessions = manager\n    ? await manager.shutdown(15000)\n    : { complete: true, pending: [] };\n  // SessionManager waits for node-pty exits, while the durable lease/node closures are asynchronous\n  // listeners of those exits. Do not let Electron exit until those exact-generation callbacks finish.\n  const releases = await waitForSessionReleases(15000);\n  const heldWorkers = workerLauncher().heldSessions();\n  const conductorHeld = conductorLaunch.leaseId ? [conductorLaunch.sessionId] : [];\n  const terminalsReleased = releases.complete && heldWorkers.length === 0 && conductorHeld.length === 0;\n  const voiceStop = sessions.complete && terminalsReleased\n    ? await conductorVoiceAuthority.stop() : null;\n  // U334: bounded, and started by teardown() above ù this await cannot outlive the gateway\'s budget.\n  const controlStop = sovereignControl ? await sovereignControl.stop() : null;\n  const gatewayExited = await waitForChildExit(gatewayProc);\n  const controlComplete = !controlStop || controlStop.closed === true;\n  const voiceComplete = !voiceStop || voiceStop.closed === true;\n  const complete = sessions.complete && terminalsReleased && gatewayExited\n    && controlComplete && voiceComplete;\n  if (!complete) {\n    log(`normal teardown incomplete: sessions=${JSON.stringify(sessions.pending)}, `\n      + `pendingReleases=${releases.pending}, heldWorkers=${JSON.stringify(heldWorkers)}, `\n      + `conductorHeld=${JSON.stringify(conductorHeld)}, gatewayExited=${gatewayExited}, `\n      + `controlStop=${JSON.stringify(controlStop)}, voiceStop=${JSON.stringify(voiceStop)}`);\n  } else {\n    log("normal teardown complete: zero managed terminal sessions; gateway exited"\n      // The control gateway\'s own outcome is stated even on the happy path: a stop that had to\n      // destroy connections, or that outlived its budget, is not a silent detail of a clean quit.\n      + `${controlStop && controlStop.was_listening\n        ? `; control gateway ${controlStop.closed ? "closed" : "TIMED OUT"}`\n          + `${controlStop.forced ? " (connections destroyed)" : ""} in ${controlStop.waited_ms} ms`\n        : ""}`);\n  }\n  await mainProcessLogger.flushAndDetach();\n  // W-18a: a teardown reached through a process FAULT never reports success, however\n  // clean the release was -- the process is dying from an unhandled fault, and exit 0\n  // would tell the launcher the opposite.\n  app.exit(faultKind || !complete ? 1 : 0);\n}\n\napp.whenReady().then(async () => {\n  registerIpc();\n  // Source the authoritative CONDUCTOR selection BEFORE the window renders pane 1 (U65), so the badge\n  // is drawn from Python from the first paint. Bounded + fail-closed, so it can never wedge startup.\n  await sourceConductorFeed();\n  // Phase 16C `.spawn`: source the governed spawn of pane 1 (spawn_conductor_pane, full live-gate\n  // chain, I-X3 counted-then-released ù D-LOOP-1) BEFORE the window renders, so pane 1\'s node_state is\n  // governed-born from the first paint. Bounded + fail-closed ù a fault leaves an honest placeholder.\n  await sourceConductorSpawn();\n  makeWindow();\n  try {\n    await bootstrap();\n  } catch (e) {\n    log(`bootstrap failed (fail-closed, no sessions will spawn): ${e.message}`);\n  }\n  // Phase 16C `.dispatch`: source the govern-born conductor\'s governed DISPATCH AFTER the window is up\n  // (it spins a bounded loopback MCP flow, mock-first ù heavier than a plain read, so it must not delay\n  // first paint). Bounded + fail-closed ù a fault leaves an honest "dispatch unavailable". Then push the\n  // refreshed conductor state so the dispatch line renders.\n  await sourceConductorDispatch();\n\n  // CP-M1 G15: live-boot readiness receipt for the shell\'s receipt_file probe.\n  // Normal start path only - under SHELL_SELFCHECK the selfcheck flow writes its own\n  // PHASE16A_SELFCHECK.json instead (paths stay distinct by design). A failure here writes\n  // nothing, so the shell probe times out and reports FAILED(TIMEOUT) honestly.\n  if (!process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK) {\n    try {\n      const fs = require("fs");\n      const receiptDir = path.join(__dirname, "..", "..", "docs", "evidence", "receipts");\n      fs.mkdirSync(receiptDir, { recursive: true });\n      fs.writeFileSync(path.join(receiptDir, "SHELL-LIVE-READY.json"),\n        JSON.stringify({ ok: true, pid: process.pid, bootedAt: new Date().toISOString() }, null, 2));\n    } catch (e) {\n      log("shell-live receipt write failed (probe will report TIMEOUT honestly): " + (e && e.message));\n    }\n  }\n  pushConductor();\n  // Phase 17A `.pty` ù CONDUCTOR-FIRST, LIVE (directive º16 track 17A / OP-8 º13.1): pane 1 runs the\n  // real interactive `claude` session on launch, so the operator lands in a conductor chat rather\n  // than a black pane. It is attempted only after supervision has had its chance to verify (the spawn\n  // itself re-checks and fails closed), it consumes exactly one governed I-X3 terminal, and it is\n  // skipped during a self-check run (which drives the launch explicitly) or when the operator sets\n  // SOW_CONDUCTOR_AUTOLAUNCH=0. A failure here never wedges startup ù the pane stays honestly dark.\n  if (!process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK && process.env.SOW_CONDUCTOR_AUTOLAUNCH !== "0") {\n    try { await launchConductorSession({ reason: "conductor-first startup" }); }\n    catch (e) { log(`conductor: auto-launch failed (fail-closed, pane 1 un-launched): ${e && e.message}`); }\n  }\n  // In-Electron pane-I/O self-check (D-P16-0 binding): when SHELL_SELFCHECK is set, drive the REAL\n  // renderer+PTY path inside this packaged runtime ù spawn a supervised pane, assert its banner\n  // renders into the xterm buffer, type a probe, assert the echo renders ù and write a\n  // machine-readable receipt. Then tear down (no orphan PTY/gateway, D-LOOP-1) and exit with the\n  // pass/fail code. This is the guard the operator required: headless Node tests alone shipped a\n  // runtime that failed at first launch; every 16A change is exercised in-runtime here.\n  if (process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK) {\n    // Which in-Electron self-check to run (D-P16-0 is per-track): SHELL_SELFCHECK=picker \u2192 16B picker;\n    // =conductor \u2192 16C conductor-selection badge; =conductor-spawn \u2192 16C governed spawn of pane 1;\n    // any other truthy value \u2192 16A pane-I/O (back-compat).\n    const kind = process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "picker" ? "picker"\n      : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "op12-picker" ? "op12-picker"\n      : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "op12-acceptance" ? "op12-acceptance"\n      : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "op12-live-acceptance" ? "op12-live-acceptance"\n      : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "op18d-registration" ? "op18d-registration"\n      : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "conductor" ? "conductor"\n        : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "conductor-spawn" ? "conductor-spawn"\n          : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "conductor-dispatch" ? "conductor-dispatch"\n            : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "statusbar" ? "statusbar"\n            : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "approvals" ? "approvals"\n              : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "recovery" ? "recovery"\n                : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "voice" ? "voice"\n                : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "voice-probe" ? "voice-probe"\n                : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "voice-mic" ? "voice-mic"\n                : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "voice-conductor" ? "voice-conductor"\n                  : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "assembled" ? "assembled"\n                    : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "conductor-launch" ? "conductor-launch"\n                      : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "conductor-pty" ? "conductor-pty"\n                        : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "conductor-roundtrip" ? "conductor-roundtrip"\n                          : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "worker-launch" ? "worker-launch"\n                            : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "worker-spawn" ? "worker-spawn"\n                            : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "pane-guards" ? "pane-guards"\n                            : process.env.SHELL_SELFCHECK === "system-pane-write" ? "ended"\n            // a pane restored from a snapshot the last shell died before it could revise: it WAS\n            // running, it is not now, and the recovery fold rewrote it rather than let this badge\n            // read `live` for a dead process (invariant 3; see layout-reconstruct)\n            : c.node_state === "session_interrupted" ? "unavailable"\n                : "selected";\n  const operationalRole = c.operational_role ? ` ╖ ${c.operational_role}` : "";\n  const task = c.task_id ? ` ╖ ${c.task_status || "ASSIGNED"}` : "";\n  badgeEl.textContent = `${c.model_label}${c.model_verified ? "" : " (unverified)"} ╖ ${c.role}`\n    + `${operationalRole}${tail} ╖ ${state}${task}`;\n  badgeEl.title = c.task_id ? `task ${c.task_id} ╖ ${c.progress_state || c.task_status || "assigned"}`\n    : (c.launch_reason || c.governed_spawn || "");\n}\n\n// Look up the live in-use count for a provider from the last status-bar model (the same governor\n// read the status bar renders). Returns null when the count is not readable (fail-closed).\nfunction statusInUse(provider) {\n  const rows = (lastStatusModel && Array.isArray(lastStatusModel.rows)) ? lastStatusModel.rows : [];\n  const r = rows.find((x) => x.provider === provider);\n  return r && typeof r.inUse === "number" ? r.inUse : null;\n}\n\n// ---- conductor-first pane chrome (º13/º12.4): CONDUCTOR badge + Resume\u2192Select control ----------\n// Only the conductor pane (main\'s pane 1) shows the model badge + succession button; every other\n// pane hides them. The badge shows the SELECTION label (fable-5), marked unverified until a live\n// reply ù never a fabricated checkpoint id (invariant 3).\nfunction applyConductorChrome(id, rec) {\n  const isConductor = !!(conductor && conductor.paneId === id);\n  rec.el.classList.toggle("conductor", isConductor);\n  const badgeEl = rec.el.querySelector(".cbadge");\n  const dispatchEl = rec.el.querySelector(".cdispatch");\n  const succEl = rec.el.querySelector(".csucc");\n  const pttEl = rec.el.querySelector(".cptt");\n  const liveEl = rec.el.querySelector(".clive");\n  const turnElOff = rec.el.querySelector(".cturn");\n  if (!isConductor) { badgeEl.textContent = ""; badgeEl.className = "cbadge"; if (dispatchEl) { dispatchEl.textContent = ""; dispatchEl.className = "cdispatch"; } succEl.style.display = "none"; if (pttEl) pttEl.style.display = "none"; if (liveEl) liveEl.style.display = "none"; if (turnElOff) { turnElOff.style.display = "none"; turnElOff.textContent = ""; turnElOff.className = "cturn"; } return; }\n  const b = conductor.badge || {};\n  const shownModel = b.displayName || b.model || "ù";\n  const provider = b.provider ? ` ╖ ${b.provider}` : "";\n  const slug = b.modelSlug ? ` ╖ ${b.modelSlug}` : "";\n  badgeEl.textContent = `CONDUCTOR ╖ ${shownModel}${provider}${slug}`\n    + `${b.verified ? "" : " (unverified)"}${b.isFallback ? " (fallback)" : ""}`\n    + ` ╖ ${conductor.nodeState || ""}`;\n  badgeEl.className = "cbadge" + (b.verified ? " verified" : " unverified");\n  // Phase 16C .dispatch: the governed dispatch line (assignments by descriptor, accepted count, legs\n  // verbatim, the OWED live-worker leg ù U58). Sourced from Python (conductor.dispatch); never a literal.\n  if (dispatchEl) {\n    const d = conductor.dispatch || {};\n    dispatchEl.textContent = d.text || "";\n    dispatchEl.className = "cdispatch" + (d.dispatched ? " ran" : " unavailable") + (d.owed ? " owed" : "");\n  }\n  // Phase 17A `.pty`: the live-session control. Hidden while a live session is RUNNING (there is\n  // nothing to start ù one governed terminal per pane); shown, with the last honest reason as its\n  // tooltip, whenever pane 1 is dark. It never reports a launch the shell did not make.\n  if (liveEl) {\n    const l = (conductor.launch || {});\n    liveEl.style.display = conductor.live ? "none" : "";\n    liveEl.textContent = l.state === "launching" ? "startingà" : "\u25b6 live";\n    liveEl.disabled = l.state === "launching";\n    liveEl.title = l.reason\n      ? `last attempt: ${l.reason}`\n      : "Run the live interactive conductor session in this pane (governed spawn)";\n  }\n  // the succession control is only shown when the affordance says it is reachable (fail-closed)\n  succEl.style.display = conductor.succession && conductor.succession.available ? "" : "none";\n  // the push-to-talk mic (º13.5) is reachable from the conductor chrome (voice-IN only; no TTS)\n  if (pttEl) pttEl.style.display = "";\n  // Phase 16E `.wire`: the VISIBLE STT-engine indicator + last-utterance badge. The engine is ALWAYS\n  // shown ("mock engine" until the WSL Parakeet path lands ù never a silent pretend-to-hear); after a\n  // capture the last outcome (delivered / queued / repeat) rides alongside it. Fail-closed to a mock tag.\n  const voiceEl = rec.el.querySelector(".cvoice");\n  if (voiceEl) {\n    const vm = voiceModel;\n    const eng = (vm && vm.control && vm.control.engine) || { mock: true, label: "mock engine" };\n    // Phase 16E `.real`: the indicator\'s label already encodes the three states (real / "<name> ready"\n    // when the real stack is installed but a stand-in used the mock / "mock engine"); trust it.\n    const engTag = eng.label || (eng.mock ? "mock engine" : (eng.name || "STT engine"));\n    const badge = vm && vm.badge;\n    const last = badge && badge.ok ? ` ╖ ${badge.label}` : (badge ? " ╖ not understood ù repeat" : "");\n    voiceEl.textContent = `\U0001f399 ${engTag}${last}`;\n    // Phase 17C `.probe` (U74): "probingà" gets its OWN class. Before this, `mock ? mock : real` styled\n    // an unanswered probe as `real` ù the label said one thing and the colour said another.\n    voiceEl.className = "cvoice" + (eng.probing ? " probing" : eng.mock ? " mock" : " real");\n    voiceEl.title = `${eng.hint || (eng.mock ? "voice transcription is a MOCK engine ù see docs/OPERATOR_NEMO_INSTALL.md" : "")}`\n      + " ù click to re-check now";\n  }\n  // Phase 17C `.disarm` (U166 / invariant 27): while a supervisor-owned voice turn is armed, this\n  // session\'s CLI is denied every tool call AND the operator\'s own typed prompts are blocked. That\n  // state was invisible and, until this sub-step, permanent ù a live pane that had simply stopped\n  // answering, with an undocumented chord as the only way back. It is drawn here from main\'s own\n  // folded indicator (the renderer decides nothing), and it carries the recovery main implements.\n  const turnEl = rec.el.querySelector(".cturn");\n  if (turnEl) {\n    const ti = (voiceModel && voiceModel.turnIndicator) || null;\n    const show = !!(ti && ti.show);\n    turnEl.style.display = show ? "" : "none";\n    turnEl.textContent = show ? `\u26d4 ${ti.label}` : "";\n    turnEl.className = "cturn" + (show ? ` show ${ti.phase}` : "");\n    turnEl.title = show ? (ti.hint || "") : "";\n  }\n}\n\n// ---- Phase 17C `.mic`: push-to-talk over the real microphone ------------------\n// One recorder for the shell (there is one operator and one conductor). `pttLabel` is the operator\'s\n// only feedback while the device is open and while a 20 s WSL transcription runs, so it always states\n// what is actually happening ù including, and especially, when it failed (U133 is the main-side half of\n// the same problem; this is the renderer\'s).\nlet micRecorder = null;\nlet pttBusy = false;\n\nfunction pttLabel(el, text, cls) {\n  if (!el) return;\n  el.textContent = text;\n  el.className = `cptt${cls ? ` ${cls}` : ""}`;\n}\n\n/**\n * Press-and-hold wiring.\n *\n * THE ONE RULE, and it is absolute: **this control never routes anything but the operator\'s own\n * recorded speech.** An earlier revision of this function fell back to the scripted stand-in ref\n * whenever capture failed, on the theory that a visible "no mic" label made the substitution honest.\n * Both mandatory reviews of this unit found that independently and they were right: `captureVoice(null)`\n * resolves to `DEFAULT_AUDIO_REF` \u2192 the MockSTT script \u2192 `"show status"` \u2192 a CHAT verdict \u2192 a real write\n * into the conductor\'s ConPTY. So an operator who pressed, SPOKE, and happened to have a muted mic would\n * have had a command they never uttered typed into a live conductor session, with a transient label as\n * the only sign. That is the exact pretend-to-hear this whole track exists to eliminate (invariant 3),\n * and it puts unauthored content in the command path (invariant 1).\n *\n * So: a capture that fails is REPORTED and routes NOTHING. The stand-in path still exists ù the\n * indicator poll and the honesty-negative self-check legs need a transcript without a microphone ù but\n * it is no longer reachable from the operator\'s talk button.\n *\n * Press-and-hold works from the pointer AND the keyboard. The button is a real `<button>`, so it is\n * focusable; Enter/Space dispatch `click` with no pointer events at all, which is why keydown/keyup are\n * wired explicitly rather than left to a `click` handler that could only ever fire after the hold ended.\n */\nfunction wirePushToTalk(el) {\n  if (!el) return;\n  const idle = () => pttLabel(el, "\U0001f3a4 talk", "");\n  // Set ONLY by a real `begin`. Without it a stray `pointerup` ù released over this button after\n  // dragging a divider or selecting text in a pane ù reached `end` and routed an utterance.\n  let held = false;\n\n  const begin = async (e) => {\n    if (e) e.stopPropagation();\n    if (pttBusy || held || (micRecorder && micRecorder.recording)) return;\n    held = true;\n    if (!window.SovereignMic) { pttLabel(el, "\U0001f3a4 no mic", "mock"); return; }\n    try {\n      micRecorder = micRecorder || new window.SovereignMic.MicRecorder();\n      await micRecorder.start();\n      // The release may have already happened: `getUserMedia` is asynchronous and a quick tap resolves\n      // `end` first, which finds nothing recording and returns. Without this the device would then\n      // open behind it and stay open ù a HOT MICROPHONE with no press holding it, and the chrome\n      // reading "listeningà" indefinitely. Found by the receipt leg added for the stand-in defect.\n      if (!held) { micRecorder.cancel(); idle(); return; }\n      pttLabel(el, "\U0001f534 listeningà", "listening");\n    } catch (err) {\n      // permission denied / no device / no Web Audio. Say so and STOP ù nothing is routed.\n      pttLabel(el, "\U0001f3a4 no mic", "mock");\n      console.warn(`push-to-talk: ${(err && err.message) || err}`);\n      micRecorder = null;\n    }\n  };\n\n  const end = async (e) => {\n    if (e) e.stopPropagation();\n    if (!held || pttBusy) return;      // no matching press \u21d2 this is not the operator talking\n    held = false;\n    if (!micRecorder || !micRecorder.recording) {\n      // the mic never opened; the label already says why. Route NOTHING.\n      pttLabel(el, "\U0001f3a4 no mic", "mock");\n      return;\n    }\n    let captured = null;\n    try {\n      captured = micRecorder.stop();\n    } catch (err) {\n      // a too-short hold, an empty buffer, a device that yielded nothing ù all reported, none routed.\n      pttLabel(el, `\U0001f3a4 ${(err && err.message) || "nothing recorded"}`, "mock");\n      console.warn(`push-to-talk: ${(err && err.message) || err}`);\n      return;\n    }\n    pttBusy = true;\n    // `.close-revalidate` (spec-audit MINOR-8): a hold that ran past MAX_CAPTURE_MS was silently\n    // truncated ù the recorder reported `capped` and the only caller dropped it, so the operator saw a\n    // normal transcription of the first minutes of what they said. The cut is now visible.\n    if (captured.capped) {\n      pttLabel(el, "\u23f3 transcribing (hold was cut short)à", "listening");\n      console.warn("push-to-talk: the hold hit the capture cap ù only the first part was recorded");\n    } else {\n      pttLabel(el, "\u23f3 transcribingà", "listening");\n    }\n    try {\n      // main writes the WAV, runs WSL Parakeet, routes the bridge\'s verdict, deletes the file.\n      const r = await S.captureVoice({ pcm: captured.pcm, sampleRate: captured.sampleRate });\n      if (r && r.sourced === false) pttLabel(el, "\U0001f3a4 voice unavailable", "mock");\n    } catch (err) {\n      pttLabel(el, "\U0001f3a4 voice unavailable", "mock");\n      console.warn(`push-to-talk: ${(err && err.message) || err}`);\n    } finally {\n      pttBusy = false;\n      await refreshVoice();\n      // the transient failure labels above are left in place for a beat so they are actually readable;\n      // any later repaint restores the idle label.\n      setTimeout(() => { if (!held && !pttBusy) idle(); }, 2500);\n    }\n  };\n\n  const cancel = (e) => {\n    if (e) e.stopPropagation();\n    if (!held) return;\n    held = false;\n    if (micRecorder) micRecorder.cancel();\n    idle();\n  };\n\n  el.addEventListener("pointerdown", begin);\n  el.addEventListener("pointerup", end);\n  el.addEventListener("pointerleave", end);\n  el.addEventListener("pointercancel", cancel);\n  // Keyboard press-and-hold, so the control is reachable without a pointing device. `repeat` is\n  // ignored: holding a key autorepeats keydown, and each one would otherwise re-enter `begin`.\n  el.addEventListener("keydown", (e) => {\n    if (e.key !== " " && e.key !== "Enter" || e.repeat) return;\n    e.preventDefault();   // stop the browser synthesising a `click` from this activation\n    begin(e);\n  });\n  el.addEventListener("keyup", (e) => {\n    if (e.key !== " " && e.key !== "Enter") return;\n    e.preventDefault();\n    end(e);\n  });\n  el.addEventListener("blur", cancel);   // focus lost mid-hold: release the device, route nothing\n  // A synthetic `click` (no pointer, no key) is NOT an utterance and must not become one.\n  el.addEventListener("click", (e) => { e.stopPropagation(); e.preventDefault(); });\n}\n\n// Phase 16E `.wire`: pull the voice-IN state (engine indicator + last-utterance badge) and repaint the\n// conductor chrome. Fail-closed: a fault leaves the last model (or the default mock indicator) in place.\nasync function refreshVoice() {\n  try { voiceModel = await S.voiceState(); } catch { /* keep last; the default is a visible mock tag */ }\n  if (conductor && conductor.paneId) applyChrome(conductor.paneId);\n}\n\n// ---- shell:layout ù the ONLY place the grid reflows -------------------------\nS.onLayout((plan) => {\n  const cells = plan.grid.cells;\n  const inGrid = new Set(cells.map((c) => c.id));\n\n  // panes no longer tiled (collapsed to a card, minimized, or destroyed): drop their live view.\n  // The session itself survives in main (byte-exact scrollback), so re-expansion reattaches.\n  for (const [id, rec] of terms) {\n    if (!inGrid.has(id)) { rec.term.dispose(); rec.el.remove(); terms.delete(id); }\n  }\n\n  grid.style.gridTemplateColumns = `repeat(${Math.max(1, plan.grid.cols)}, 1fr)`;\n  grid.style.gridTemplateRows = `repeat(${Math.max(1, plan.grid.rows)}, 1fr)`;\n\n  // (re)place panes in cell order; P0/P1 keep their double (span-2) cell.\n  for (const c of cells) {\n    const rec = ensurePane(c.id);\n    rec.el.style.gridColumn = c.span === 2 ? "span 2" : "span 1";\n    rec.el.classList.toggle("pinned", c.priority === "P0");\n    rec.el.dataset.priority = c.priority;\n    grid.appendChild(rec.el); // reorder to match the plan\n    applyChrome(c.id);\n  }\n\n  // right-hand status-card rail: P2 overflow + P3/P4, never P0/P1 (º10.2 hard rule enforced in main).\n  rail.innerHTML = plan.cards.length\n    ? plan.cards.map((c) => {\n        const info = meta.get(c.id) || {};\n        // W-36 (R-54): every value here is model- or system-supplied ù pane titles come from the\n        // launch chrome, from provider/model identifiers, and from `spec.title` on the pane-creation\n        // path. `esc()` is defined in this file and was already used by the inspector panels below;\n        // these two sinks were simply missed. Escaped at the interpolation rather than at the\n        // source, because this is the boundary where the text becomes markup.\n        return `<div class="railcard" data-id="${esc(c.id)}" data-priority="${esc(c.priority)}">\n          <div class="rc-id">${esc(info.title || c.id)}</div>\n          <div class="rc-meta">${esc(c.priority)} ╖ ${esc(info.sessionState || "ù")}</div></div>`;\n      }).join("")\n    : `<div class="rail-empty">no collapsed panes</div>`;\n  rail.querySelectorAll(".railcard").forEach((el) => el.addEventListener("click", () => S.focus(el.dataset.id)));\n\n  for (const { fit } of terms.values()) { try { fit.fit(); } catch { /* not attached */ } }\n});\n\n// ---- shell:state ù chrome/content restyle, no reflow ------------------------\nS.onState((state) => {\n  focusedId = state.focusedId;\n  maximizedId = state.maximizedId;\n\n  meta.clear();\n  for (const p of state.visible) meta.set(p.id, { title: p.title, pinned: p.pinned });\n  for (const s of state.sessions) {\n    const m = meta.get(s.id) || {};\n    m.sessionState = s.state;\n    meta.set(s.id, m);\n  }\n\n  for (const id of terms.keys()) applyChrome(id);\n\n  // Phase 16A: keep DOM keyboard focus on the operator\'s focused pane so typing reaches its session\n  // (the focus model is authoritative; the xterm textarea must actually hold focus to emit onData).\n  if (focusedId) { const fr = terms.get(focusedId); if (fr) fr.term.focus(); }\n\n  // status bar cards (shell is observable ù invariant 27). The supervision card reflects the\n  // recovery lifecycle: SUPERVISED (READY) / DEGRADED / RECOVERING, with the supervision epoch.\n  const rec = state.recovery || { state: state.supervised ? "SUPERVISED" : "INIT", epoch: 0 };\n  const sup = document.getElementById("card-sup");\n  sup.textContent = state.supervised ? "READY" : (rec.state === "INIT" ? "DENIED" : rec.state);\n  sup.className = "v" + (state.supervised ? "" : " bad");\n  renderRecoveryBanner(rec);\n  document.getElementById("card-sessions").textContent = String(state.sessions.length);\n  document.getElementById("card-focus").textContent = state.focusedId || "ù";\n\n  const min = document.getElementById("minimized");\n  min.innerHTML = state.minimized.length\n    // W-36 (R-54): same sink class as the rail above ù a minimized pane\'s title is the same\n    // model-supplied string, rendered through innerHTML.\n    ? "minimized: " + state.minimized.map((m) => `<span data-id="${esc(m.id)}">${esc(m.title || m.id)}</span>`).join("")\n    : "";\n  min.querySelectorAll("span").forEach((s) => s.addEventListener("click", () => S.focus(s.dataset.id)));\n});\n\n// ---- recovery banner (directive º9 track 14A) -------------------------------\n// Live supervision state rides shell:state.recovery; interrupted sessions from a PRIOR shell run\n// arrive once on shell:recovery. Both are surfaced honestly: a lost channel reads DEGRADED and\n// blocks new sessions; interrupted sessions are reported for supervised relaunch, never respawned.\nlet interruptedNotice = null;\nfunction renderRecoveryBanner(rec) {\n  const el = document.getElementById("recovery");\n  if (rec && rec.state === "DEGRADED") {\n    el.className = "show degraded";\n    el.textContent = `\u26a0 control-plane channel LOST ù sessions torn down, new sessions blocked (fail-closed). Reconnectingà`;\n    return;\n  }\n  if (rec && rec.state === "RECOVERING") {\n    el.className = "show recovering";\n    el.textContent = `\u21bb reconnecting to the control plane ù admission stays closed until the channel re-verifiesà`;\n    return;\n  }\n  // healthy: show a lingering interrupted-session notice if one is pending, else hide.\n  if (interruptedNotice) { el.className = "show interrupted"; el.innerHTML = interruptedNotice; }\n  else el.className = "";\n}\n\nS.onRecovery((info) => {\n  // Phase 15E .recovery: the conductor-first layout was reconstructed on boot (pinned CONDUCTOR\n  // pane 1 + reattaching worker panes). Surface it honestly, plus any interrupted sessions that\n  // need SUPERVISED relaunch (never auto-respawned).\n  const layout = info && info.layout;\n  // Phase 16D `.recovery` (U68): restore each worker pane\'s governed model BADGE from the\n  // reconstructed chrome so a restarted shell repaints the exact selection instead of a blank pane\n  // (paneBadges starts empty in a fresh renderer). Honest: this is a RECORDED selection restored,\n  // and no live node is claimed ù but that is the FOLD\'s doing, not this loop\'s. It used to say\n  // "node_state stays awaiting-governed-spawn", which stopped being true at 17B `.spawn`: a\n  // snapshot written by a shell that was killed carries `running`, and `applyModelBadge` renders\n  // `running` as **live**. `reconstructLayout` now rewrites a stale live state to\n  // `session_interrupted` before it ever reaches here (invariants 3/27).\n  if (layout && Array.isArray(layout.panes)) {\n    for (const w of layout.panes) {\n      if (w && w.paneId && w.chrome && w.chrome.model_label) {\n        paneBadges.set(w.paneId, w.chrome);\n        if (terms.has(w.paneId)) applyChrome(w.paneId);\n      }\n    }\n  }\n  const list = (info && info.interrupted) || (layout ? layout.interrupted || [] : []);\n  const relaunchIds = list.map((s) => (typeof s === "string" ? s : s.id));\n  if (!relaunchIds.length && !layout) { interruptedNotice = null; return; }\n  const parts = [];\n  if (layout) {\n    parts.push(`\u2713 conductor-first layout restored ù CONDUCTOR pane 1 pinned, ${layout.summary.workers} worker pane(s), `\n      + `admission ${layout.admissionOpen ? "OPEN" : "SHUT (awaiting a verified channel)"}`);\n  }\n  if (relaunchIds.length) {\n    parts.push(`<span class="r-relaunch">\u21ba ${relaunchIds.length} pane(s)/session(s) need supervised relaunch (no naked re-spawn): `\n      + relaunchIds.map((id) => esc(String(id))).join(", ") + `</span>`);\n  }\n  interruptedNotice = parts.join(" ╖ ");\n  const el = document.getElementById("recovery");\n  el.className = "show interrupted";\n  el.innerHTML = interruptedNotice;\n});\n\nS.onData((id, data, seq) => {\n  const rec = terms.get(id);\n  if (!rec) return; // no view for this pane right now; scrollback replays it on next (re)attach\n  // While (re)attaching, the feed buffers the chunk (returns null); once scrollback is replayed it\n  // passes straight through. This is what stops early PTY output (the banner) being dropped.\n  const w = rec.feed.live(seq, data);\n  if (w !== null) rec.term.write(w);\n});\n\nS.onLog((line) => console.log(line));\n\n// conductor-first pane (º13/º12.4): store the CONDUCTOR badge + Resume\u2192Select state and restyle the\n// panes that already exist (chrome-only; no reflow). The conductor pane may not be built yet on the\n// first push ù applyChrome re-reads `conductor` when the pane\'s view is created by the layout plan.\nS.onConductor((state) => { conductor = state; for (const id of terms.keys()) applyChrome(id); refreshVoice(); });\n// Phase 17C `.probe` (U74): main pushes the voice state when the ASYNCHRONOUS STT probe settles. The\n// badge flips from "probingà" to the real answer with no operator action ù the probe answers on its own\n// schedule (7û18 s here), and a chrome that only repainted on click would keep showing a stale question.\nS.onVoice((state) => {\n  if (!state) return;\n  voiceModel = state;\n  if (conductor && conductor.paneId) applyChrome(conductor.paneId);\n});\n// Phase 17B `.spawn`: main revised a worker pane\'s governed chrome (its session ended) ù repaint the\n// badge from what main sent, never from what this renderer last assumed (invariant 3/27).\nS.onPaneChrome((id, chrome) => {\n  if (!id || !chrome) return;\n  paneBadges.set(id, chrome);\n  if (terms.has(id)) applyChrome(id);\n});\n\ndocument.getElementById("btn-new").addEventListener("click", async () => {\n  try { await S.newPane({}); }\n  catch (e) { console.error("new pane refused:", e); }\n});\n\n// ---- routing/artifact inspector drawer (Plan º10.3) -------------------------\n// A THIN VIEW: it pulls governed MCP state on demand via the read-only bridge and renders the\n// per-task model (context routed / artifacts published / gate chain) the pure core already\n// folded. It computes nothing and decides nothing. Operator-run surface ù headlessly the DATA\n// path is proven (apps/desktop/test/inspector-source.test.js); this painting is operator-verified.\nconst esc = (s) => String(s == null ? "" : s).replace(/[&<>"]/g, (c) => (\n  { "&": "&amp;", "<": "&lt;", ">": "&gt;", \'"\': "&quot;" }[c]));\n\nfunction renderInspector(res) {\n  const body = document.getElementById("insp-body");\n  // U336 (19.7): `ok` is now the AND of the two reads, so a FALSE `ok` no longer means "nothing to\n  // show" ù one half may be perfectly readable. Bail only when there is nothing readable at all;\n  // otherwise paint what there is, under a banner naming what could not be read. What must never\n  // happen again is the third case: a failure painted as counts.\n  const readable = Boolean(res && (res.model || (res.operational && res.operational.available === true)));\n  if (!res || !readable) {\n    body.innerHTML = `<div class="insp-err">inspector unavailable ù ${esc(res && res.error) || "fail-closed"}</div>`;\n    return;\n  }\n  const degraded = res.ok === true ? "" : `<div class="insp-err">partially unreadable ù ${esc(res.error) || "fail-closed"}</div>`;\n  // Both halves obey the same rule: null is unreadable, and an unreadable half prints no counts.\n  const legacyAvailable = Boolean(res.model && res.summary);\n  const model = res.model || { tasks: [], unattributed: { artifacts: [], gates: [] }, anomalies: [] };\n  const summary = res.summary || {};\n  const routingReadable = res.routingReadable;\n  // An unreadable live-orchestration feed carries NO counts (operational-source.js): `summary`,\n  // `tasks`, `messages` and `debates` are null, not empty. Rows are plan\n// approvals / protected actions / clarifications with a badge count. The Approve/Reject buttons only\n// RECORD the operator\'s request (invariant 1 ù the governed resolve is Python-side); Approve is shown\n// only for an approvable row (a gate-failed plan is not approvable ù invariant 16). The pure fold that\n// produced these rows is headlessly proven (terminal/test/approval-drawer.test.js).\nfunction setApprovalBadge(count) {\n  const b = document.getElementById("appr-badge");\n  if (!b) return;\n  b.textContent = count > 0 ? "inline-block" : "none";\n}\n\nfunction renderApprovals(res) {\n  const body = document.getElementById("appr-body");\n  if (!res || res.ok !== true) {\n    body.innerHTML = `<div class="insp-err">approvals unavailable ù ${esc(res && res.error) || "fail-closed"}</div>`;\n    setApprovalBadge(0);\n    return;\n  }\n  setApprovalBadge(res.badgeCount || 0);\n  const row = (r) => `<div class="appr-item appr-${esc(r.kind)}">\n    <div class="ins-title">${esc(r.kindLabel)} <span class="dim">${esc(r.origin)}</span></div>\n    <div class="ins-ctx">${esc(r.summary)}</div>\n    <div class="appr-actions">\n      ${r.approvable ? `<button class="appr-approve" data-id="${esc(r.id)}">Approve</button>` : ""}\n      <button class="appr-reject" data-id="${esc(r.id)}">${r.approvable ? The governed outcome is\n// now rendered, including what is still owed, and a governed REFUSAL says why the row stayed.\nlet lastApprovalOutcome = null;\n\nfunction approvalOutcomeLine() {\n  if (!lastApprovalOutcome) return "";\n  const o = lastApprovalOutcome;\n  return `<div class="ins-ctx appr-outcome">${esc(o)}</div>`;\n}\n\nasync function decideApproval(itemId, decision) {\n  // The shell records the operator\'s intent; the governed resolve is Python-side (invariant 1).\n  try {\n    const res = await S.decideApproval(itemId, decision, "");\n    const g = res && res.governed;\n    if (!res || res.routed !== true || !g) {\n      lastApprovalOutcome = `${decision} of ${itemId} could not be routed to the authority ù nothing was decided (${(res && res.error) || "fail-closed"})`;\n    } else if (g.resolved === true) {\n      const owed = g.side_effects_owed && g.side_effects_owed.note;\n      lastApprovalOutcome = `${decision}d ${itemId} ù recorded by the governed authority`\n        + (decision === "approve" && owed ? `. NOT YET EXECUTED: ${owed}` : ".");\n    } else if (g.refused === true) {\n      lastApprovalOutcome = `${decision} of ${itemId} was REFUSED by the authority ù ${g.reason || "no reason given"}`;\n    } else {\n      lastApprovalOutcome = `${decision} of ${itemId} is unresolved ù ${g.reason || "the authority was unavailable"}`;\n    }\n  } catch (e) {\n    lastApprovalOutcome = `${decision} of ${itemId} failed at the shell boundary ù nothing was decided (${(e && e.message) || "error"})`;\n  }\n  refreshApprovals();\n}\n\nasync function refreshApprovals() {\n  const body = document.getElementById("appr-body");\n  body.innerHTML = \'<div class="dim">reading approvalsà</div>\';\n  try { renderApprovals(await S.approvals()); }\n  catch (e) { renderApprovals({ ok: false, error: (e && e.message) || "read failed" }); }\n}\n\ndocument.getElementById("btn-approvals").addEventListener("click", () => {\n  const drawer = document.getElementById("approvals");\n  const open = drawer.classList.toggle("open");\n  if (open) refreshApprovals();\n});\ndocument.getElementById("appr-refresh").addEventListener("click", refreshApprovals);\ndocument.getElementById("appr-close").addEventListener("click", () => document.getElementById("approvals").classList.remove("open"));\nS.onApprovals((model) => setApprovalBadge((model && model.badgeCount) || 0));\n\n// ---- subscription-concurrency status bar (directive º11/15A; Phase 16D `.statusbar`) --------\n// A THIN VIEW of the governor n/allowance count. It grants NO new authority (no Node, no PTY, no socket):\n// everything here is already reachable from this renderer. Armed ONLY for the diagnostic run (the\n// main process passes ?selfcheck=1 under SHELL_SELFCHECK), so it is absent in a normal operator\n// launch. The main-process self-check drives it via executeJavaScript to assert the banner/echo\n// really rendered into the pane buffer.\nif (new URLSearchParams(window.location.search).get("selfcheck") === "1") {\n  // The U177 channel sweep, fetched only when a receipt asks for it. `script-src \'self\'` allows this\n  // exactly as it allows the tags in index.html; the module registers itself on `window` and needs a\n  // bridge INJECTED before it can do anything, so loading it grants nothing either way ù the reason\n  // it is not in index.html is that a normal launch has no consumer for it.\n  let channelSweepLoad = null;\n  const loadChannelSweep = () => {\n    if (window.SovereignChannelSweep) return Promise.resolve(window.SovereignChannelSweep);\n    if (!channelSweepLoad) {\n      channelSweepLoad = new Promise((resolve, reject) => {\n        const el = document.createElement("script");\n        el.src = "channel-sweep.js";\n        el.onload = () => (window.SovereignChannelSweep\n          ? resolve(window.SovereignChannelSweep)\n          : reject(new Error("channel-sweep.js loaded but registered nothing")));\n        el.onerror = () => reject(new Error("channel-sweep.js could not be loaded"));\n        document.head.appendChild(el);\n      });\n    }\n    return channelSweepLoad;\n  };\n  window.__sovereignSelfCheck = {\n    hasTerm(id) { return terms.has(id);
